```
def mirror_descent_opt(a, time_limit, beta0, beta1, eta0, topk, verbose):
    a = norm_sum1(a)
    best=eval true
    y=a.copy()
    t=0
    while time:
       c=conv(y,y)
       w=softmax(beta*(c-cmax)) maybe top
       g=grad(w,y)
       # maybe normalize gradient so max abs =1 or mean?
       # For mirror step, choose step = alpha / sqrt(t) * scale
       for step in [eta0/sqrt(t), ...]:
          z = y * exp(-step*g)
          if z<=0... normalize
          F=conv(z,z).max()
          if F < current_F - tol: break
       if improved: update y with momentum? 
```

Let's consider implementing custom L-BFGS. It could be robust and time-controlled. But SciPy easier. We need no closures. Let's design global state for objective:
```
OBJ_N = 1
OBJ_BETA = 100.0
OBJ_ALPHA = 2.0
OBJ_CONST = 1.0? 
def obj_val_grad(x):
    n=OBJ_N; beta=OBJ_BETA
    # x length n-1, last logfixed 0
    logits = np.empty(n)
    logits[:-1]=x; logits[-1]=0.0
    m=logits.max(); exp=np.exp(logits-m); a=exp/exp.sum()
    c=fast_conv(a,a)
    # smoothed max over c
    m_c=c.max()
    # if m_c <=0 etc
    # L = m_c + log(sum(exp(beta*(c-m_c))))/beta
    # To avoid underflow: t=beta*(c-m_c), s=sum(exp(t))
    L = m_c + np.log(s)/beta
    f = 2*n*L
    # gradient
    w = np.exp(t - np.log(s))
    g = 2*active_grad_from_weights(w,a)
    dlogit = g - np.dot(a,g)  # length n
    gx = a[:-1]*dlogit[:-1]
    return f, gx
```

Mirror line search details:
```
def mirror_search(a, time_limit, lr0=0.5, beta_rel=50.0, topk_frac=0.2, use_exact=False):
   best_a=norm_sum1(a); best_m=fast_conv(best_a).max(); best_J=2n*best_m
   y=best_a; stall=0
   t=0
   while time < limit:
      c=fast_conv(y); my=c.max(); J=2n*my
      if J<best_J: update best
      # weights
      if use_exact:
         k=min(int(topk_frac*(2n-1)), len(c)); if k<1 k=1
         idx=np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
         w=np.zeros(len(c)); w[idx]=1/k
      else:
         w=softmax_weights(c, beta_rel/my if my>0 else 1)
      g=grad_from_weights(w,y)
      # normalize gradient by max abs or mean? use inf
      gscale=np.max(np.abs(g)); if gscale<1e-30: gscale=1
      gnorm=g/gscale
      # momentum in log? We can maintain p = momentum * p + grad? But step adaptive.
      # Try steps descending
      accepted=False
      for lr in [lr0, lr0*0.5, lr0*0.25, lr0*0.125, 1e-6]:
         logz=np.log(np.maximum(y,1e-300)) - lr*gnorm
         logz -= logz.max()
         z=np.exp(logz); z=np.maximum(z,0); zsum=z.sum(); if zsum>0: z/=zsum
         Fz=fast_conv(z).max(); Jz=2n*Fz
         if Jz < J + 1e-15: # or best? If local max maybe accept if < best
             z_prev=z; break
      if accepted: y=z; maybe y = norm(best + (1-moment)*(...))? else reduce lr0? 
      if not accepted: # perturb
```

def eval_fast(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); a=np.maximum(a,0); s=a.sum(); if s<1e-12: return inf
    c=fast_conv(a); return float(2.0*a.size*c.max()/(s*s))

def lbfgs_polish(a, time_limit, rel_betas=(200, 1000, 3000)):
   a=orient(a); best=eval_fast
   x = np.log(np.maximum(a[1:]/a[0],1e-300))
   for rel in rel_betas:
      if time expired break
      _OPT_N=n; _OPT_RELBETA=rel
      maxiter = 150
      res = sopt.minimize(_lb_fun, x, jac=_lb_jac, method='L-BFGS-B', options={'maxiter':maxiter, 'ftol':1e-14, 'gtol':1e-10})
      x_new = res.x if finite else x
      a_new = softmax_from_x(x_new)
      update best
      x = x_new (with a0 fixed? Need derive x from a_new: log(a_new[1:]/a_new[0]))
   return best
```

Mirror optimization `mirror_opt` details:
```
def mirror_opt(a, time_limit, lr0=1.0, rel_beta_seq=(10,50,200,1000), top_frac=0.2, exact_after=True, verbose=False):
   a=norm_sum1(orient(a)); best=a; best_m=convmax; best_J=2n*best_m
   y=a.copy()
   t=0; stall=0; lr=lr0
   phase_idx cycles through rel_beta
   while elapsed<time_limit:
      c=fast_conv(y); my=c.max(); J=2n*my
      if J < best_J: update best
      # select weights
      phase = ... maybe if exact_after and t>some: use top exact; else softmax rel
      if exact:
         k=max(50, int(top_frac*(2n-1)))
         idx=np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
         w=np.zeros_like(c); w[idx]=1.0/k
      else:
         w=softmax_weights(c, rel)
      g=grad_from_weights(w,y); gscale=max(abs(g)); if <eps: break
      gnorm=g/gscale
      # adaptive step: try current lr then dec
      accepted=False
      for mult in [1,0.5,0.25,0.125]:
         step=lr*mult
         logz=np.log(np.maximum(y,1e-300)) - step*gnorm
         # If step large, logz may underflow; center
         logz -= logz.max()
         z=np.exp(logz); zsum=z.sum(); if zsum>0: z/=zsum
         Fz=fast_conv(z).max(); Jz=2n*Fz
         if Jz < best_J or Jz <= my*(1-1e-12):
             z_prev=z; accepted=True; break
      if accepted:
         # optional momentum: y = norm(0.9*z +0.1*y?) in log? We can just set y=z.
         y=z_prev
         stall=0
      else:
         lr *= 0.5
         if lr < 1e-4:
             # perturb best
             rng = rng random state maybe top-level? We can use np.random.default_rng(t)
             y = perturb(best, sigma=0.1)
             lr=0.2
         stall +=1
      if stall > 2000: etc
      if t % 2000 < phase_change: update rel? 
      t +=1
   return best
```

Let's accept if `Jz < my - 1e-12` or `Jz < best_J`. Since my may equal best. If at local plateau where subgradient doesn't decrease, line search fails, then perturb. Could be many fails. We can also accept small increases if using smoothed? But true objective may increase temporarily; we only want improve best. For local optimization, accept if `Jz <= my * (1 + 1e-9)` (nonincrease) to ensure no drift; then y=z. If it's equal due roundoff, okay. If no nonincrease, perturb.

But if gradient subgradient not descent due nonconvex, may stagnate. L-BFGS may be better.

def lp_step(y, active_idx, K_limit, verbose=False):
   n=len(y)
   K=len(active_idx); if K==0: return None
   # Build dense A_ub shape K x (n+1)
   A = np.zeros((K,n+1)); A[:,n] = -1.0
   for r,k in enumerate(active_idx):
      k=int(k)
      start=max(0,k-n+1); end=min(n,k+1)
      if start<end:
         idx=np.arange(start,end)
         A[r,:end]??? assign A[r,idx]=y[k-idx]
   c_obj = np.zeros(n+1); c_obj[n]=1
   A_eq = np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,:n]=1
   bounds = [(0,None)]*n + [(0,None)]
   res = linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.zeros(K), A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=[1], bounds=bounds, method='highs')
   if res.status not in (0,1?): b=res.x[:n]; clip; normalize; return b
```

def _lb_compute(x):
    n=_LB_N; rel=_LB_REL
    x=np.asarray(x,float)
    logits=np.empty(n,float)
    logits[0]=0.0
    logits[1:]=x
    mx=logits.max()

    a=e/e.sum()
    c=fast_conv(a)
    m=c.max()
    if m <= 0: return 0.0, np.zeros(n-1)
    beta=rel/m
    t=beta*(c-m)
    # For stability, if beta > 1e8? m small but rel/m maybe 1e9; t <=0, exp(-700) under. ok.
    et=np.exp(t)
    s=et.sum()
    if s <=0 or not finite: # fallback
        u=np.zeros(n-1); L=m; g=...
    log_s=np.log(s)
    L=m + log_s/beta
    w=et/s
    g=grad_conv_weights(w,a)
    dot=np.dot(a,g)
    gx=a[1:]*(g[1:]-dot)
    # optionally clip gx

def _lb_fun(x):
    val,_=_lb_compute(x); return val
def _lb_jac(x):
    _,grad=_lb_compute(x); return grad

General fixed idx:
- `x` length n-1 corresponds to indices in order `idxs = [i for i in range(n) if i != fixed]`.
- To build logits: `logits=np.empty(n); logits[fixed]=0; logits[idxs]=x`. Filling each call with `logits[idxs]=x` O(n) using precomputed idxs global.
- Gradient wrt x: `gx = a[idxs] * (g[idxs] - dot)`.
Set globals `_LB_IDS` and `_LB_FIXED`. For fixed=0, ids=range(1,n). For arbitrary, compute once. This avoids orientation issues. We can choose fixed index = argmax(a) for each run. Let's detail each function.

`fast_conv`:
```
def fast_conv(a):
    a=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    if n==1: return a.copy()
    size=2*n-1
    L=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
    Af=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(Af*Af, L)[:size]
    # remove tiny negative
    c[c < 0.0] = 0.0
    return c
```
Potential if a contains inf? We norm before. For speed, local variables.

`softmax_weights`:
```
def softmax_weights(c, rel_beta):
    m=c.max()
    if m <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(c.size, dtype=np.float64)/c.size
    beta = rel_beta / m
    t = beta*(c-m)
    # t max 0, min maybe -inf; exp safe
    et = np.exp(t)
    s = et.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
        # if all underflow except? actually exp(0)=1 so s>=1 unless c all -inf
        idx = np.argmax(c); w=np.zeros_like(c); w[idx]=1; return w
    w = et / s
    return w
```
If rel_beta very small? okay.

`grad_conv_weights`:
```
def grad_conv_weights(w,a):
    n=a.size; m=w.size
    if n==1: return np.array([2.0*np.dot(w,a[0])?])? Actually c=[a0^2], derivative 2a0 wrt a0. For general n>1.
    size=m+n-1
    L=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
    W=np.fft.rfft(w,L); Ar=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1],L)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(W*Ar,L)[:size]
    g=2.0*conv[n-1:2*n-1]
    g[g<0]=0
    return g
```
For n=1: size=1? w length1, a length1, conv length1, slice 0:1 -> [w0*?]; 2? Our FFT: W= w, Ar=a[::-1] (a0), conv=w*a0; g=2*conv[0:1]=2 w0 a0. Since w sums1, g=2a0; derivative of c=a0^2 wrt a0=2a0. Good. Works for n=1.

`eval_fast`:
```
def eval_fast(a):
   a=np.maximum(np.asarray(a,float),0); s=a.sum(); n=a.size
   if s<1e-12: return inf
   if n==0: return inf
   c=fast_conv(a); m=c.max(); return float(2.0*n*m/(s*s))
```
Maybe if a has inf/nan: check finite first.

`eval_official(a)`:
```
def eval_official(a):
   try:
      arr=np.asarray(a,float)
      arr=np.clip(arr,0,1000)
      lst=[float(x) for x in arr]
      val=float(evaluate_sequence(lst))
      return val
   except Exception: return inf
```
This can be heavy for n large but final few times. For updating best we use eval_fast, but final official. Also when checking initial parent, use official to compare exact (clipping). Since we normalize sum1, clipping not matter. But if a0 maybe >1000? No.

`norm_sum1`:
```
def norm_sum1(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).copy()
   a[a<0]=0
   a[~np.isfinite(a)]=0
   s=a.sum()
   if s<1e-200: return np.ones(a.size)/a.size
   return a/s
```

`orient_endpoint`:
```
def orient_endpoint(a):
   if a.size>=2 and a[-1] > a[0]: return a[::-1].copy()
   return a.copy()
```
Maybe if a[0]+? For LP no need; for L-BFGS fixed max not endpoint. We can use fixed max, no orientation required. But central weighted with x assumes orientation; parent could be reversed; objective symmetric, use orientation with larger a0 for continuity; but L-BFGS fixed max. Mirror no orientation. Use orientation for parametric maybe.

`central_binomial(n)`:
```
def central_binomial(n):
   b=np.ones(n)
   if n>1:
       k=np.arange(1,n,dtype=np.float64)
       ratios=(2*k-1)/(2*k) # for k index 1:1/2
       b[1:]=np.cumprod(ratios)
   return norm_sum1(b)
```
Check ratio formula: for k=1 ratio (2*1-1)/(2*1)=0.5. Good.

Parametric weighted central:
```
def weighted_central(n, alpha, beta=0.0, sigma=0.3):
   a=central_binomial(n)
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   w=1.0 + alpha*x
   if beta !=0: w += beta*np.exp(-((x-1.0)/sigma)**2)
   w=np.maximum(w,1e-6)
   a=a*w
   return norm_sum1(a)
```
But calling central_binomial normalizes; if we use weights after normalization, okay. Better generate unnormalized b then weight; `norm_sum1` after.

Recurrence:
```
def profile_sequence(n, delta, mode):
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   if mode==0: q=x
   elif mode==1: q=x*x
   elif mode==2: q=np.sin(0.5*np.pi*x)**2
   elif mode==3: q=np.maximum(x - 0.2,0)**2 maybe? 
   c = 1.0 - delta*q
   c[0]=1.0
   # maybe c must not go negative; delta<=0.8
   a=np.zeros(n); a[0]=1.0
   worst=0.0
   for k in range(1,n):
       if k>1:
           s=np.dot(a[1:k], a[k-1:0:-1])
       else: s=0.0
       val=0.5*(c[k]-s)
       if val < -1e-8:
           # if small negative, set 0; if large, fail
           if val < -1e-5: return None
           val=0.0
       elif val < 0: val=0.0
       a[k]=val
   if a.sum() <=0: return None
   return norm_sum1(a)
```
But this recurrence with arbitrary c may produce tail full convolution huge. Evaluate. Use modes and δ [0.05,0.15,0.3,0.5]. O(n^2) for n maybe 5000; for many candidates too slow. For short initial, central/weighted enough. We can include profile for n≈5200/6000.

Start A = [1,0,...]. Iterate at least 2 log2 n times? At step with length m (power of 2), update A_new = (A + C_m / A)/2 truncated to length 2m. Need series division. def optimize_start(a, time_left, allow_lp=True):
   t0=time.time(); deadline=t0+time_left
   a=norm_sum1(a); best=...
   # mirror
   tm = min(time_left*0.35, 180.0)
   if tm>20: a2=mirror_opt(a, tm, exact_mix=True); best=...
   # lbfgs
   tl = min(remaining*0.35, 250)
   if sopt and tl>20: a2=lbfgs_polish(a, tl); best=...
   # lp
   if allow_lp and a.size<=10000:
      tlp=min(remaining*0.35, 300)
      if tlp>60: a2=lp_polish(a, tlp); best=...
   # short lbfgs if remaining>30

```

Need choose mirror parameters for different n. `mirror_opt` time limit; inside loop time check. Let's design with phases:
```
def mirror_opt(a, time_limit, lr0=0.8, rel_betas=(10.0,50.0,200.0), top_frac=0.15, verbose=False):
   a=norm_sum1(orient_endpoint(a))
   best=a.copy(); best_m=fast_conv(best).max(); best_J=2*n*best_m
   y=a.copy()
   t=0; base_lr=lr0
   while time.monotonic? use time.time() < t0+time_limit:
      c=fast_conv(y); my=c.max(); Jy=2*n*my
      if Jy < best_J: updates (but best_m normalized? If y sum1; best keep normalized)
      # phase: if t < 1000 use rel_betas[0], 1000-3000 rel[1], else exact top
      # Actually exact top maybe better after some smoothing.
      if t < 500: rel=rel_betas[0]; exact=False
      elif t <2000: rel=rel_betas[1]; exact=False
      else: rel=rel_betas[2]; exact=True maybe every other
      if exact:
        weights top exact
      else: weights softmax rel
      g=...
      if not finite: break
      gscale=max(abs); if small break
      gnorm=g/gscale
      accepted=False
      for mult in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625):
         step=base_lr*mult
         if step<=1e-6: continue
         logz=np.log(np.maximum(y,1e-300)) - step*gnorm
         logz-=logz.max()
         z=np.exp(logz); zsum=z.sum(); if zsum>0: z/=zsum
         Fz=fast_conv(z).max(); Jz=2*n*Fz
         if Jz < best_J - 1e-14 or Jz <= Jy*(1.0-1e-12):
             y=z; accepted=True; break
      if not accepted:
         base_lr *= 0.7
         if base_lr < 0.02:
            # random restart around best
            sigma=0.15
            y=perturb_sequence(best, sigma, t)
            base_lr=0.5
      t += 1
      if verbose and t%5000==0: print
   # final evaluate best fast, maybe official not.
   return best
```
Concern: `Jz <= Jy*(1-1e-12)` may be too strict if y not best and Jy > best? If Jy not best, we might want accept moves that improve relative y even if not best. We compute Jy current y; if y is worse than best due to previous accept? We always only accept decreases, so y's J should be <= previous y but maybe > best? We update best to min, y may be not best if line search accepted a move that didn't improve best but decreased y? It could still be > best? If y started from best, and only accept relative decrease, Jy should never exceed starting best? Actually if accept Jz <= Jy, then sequence nonincreasing; so Jy<=initial J. best is min of accepted y, so best<=Jy. Good. If y gets perturbed to best, okay. So relative decrease fine.
If plateau no decrease, perturbation.
But because we use normalized gradient step, the first-order decrease may be tiny <1e-12 at near optimum. Then stalls. For polish, L-BFGS/LP can handle. Mirror mainly to move.

`perturb_sequence` uses seed. Need not update best from perturbed y unless it improves later. If y perturbed to a worse sequence, line search may decrease from there; best remains.

Mirror maybe could waste time if no improvement. But okay.

`lbfgs_polish` parameters:

def lbfgs_polish(a, time_limit, rel_betas=(200.0, 1000.0, 3000.0), maxiter=150, verbose=False):
   a=norm_sum1(a)
   best=a.copy()
   best_J=eval_fast(a)
   x=None
   fixed=np.argmax(a)
   ids=np.concatenate? Build:
      idx_arr=np.arange(a.size)
      idx_arr=np.delete(idx_arr, fixed)
   _LB_N=n; _LB_FIXED=fixed; _LB_IDS=idx_arr; _LB_REL=...
   x = np.log(np.maximum(a[idx_arr]/a[fixed], 1e-300))
   for rel in rel_betas:
      if time expired break
      _LB_REL=rel
      x = np.clip(x, -60, 60)
      res=sopt.minimize(_lb_fun, x, jac=_lb_jac, method='L-BFGS-B', options={'maxiter':maxiter, 'ftol':1e-14, 'gtol':1e-10})
      if res.x finite:
         x_new=np.clip(res.x, -60, 60)
         a_new=logits_to_a(x_new, fixed, ids)
         sc=eval_fast(a_new)
         if sc<best_J: best=a_new; best_J=sc
         # Also if res improved smooth, use x_new for next even if true not? use if finite.
         x = x_new
      # Update fixed? Keep same fixed to maintain x. If fixed coordinate becomes extremely small, x may large; maybe reset fixed to argmax(best) and x from best for next beta.
      if best_J < eval_fast(a): a=norm_sum1(best)
   # convert final x? return best
```
If fixed coordinate becomes small, x entries can be huge positive (a_other/a_fixed). We clip to 60; but true optimum may have ratios >e^60? Not likely. If best after run moved fixed coordinate small, next beta reset fixed? We can reset after each beta to argmax(a_best), and x from a_best. That's safer. But L-BFGS run result may have good sequence but not official; after each beta, if best improved, set a=best. For next beta, choose fixed=argmax(a) and x accordingly. If no improvement, still use x from result? We can choose a = result_a if finite and eval not much worse? To continue smooth descent, use result_a. But if true score worse, maybe still on smooth path. Use result_a if finite; if eval > best*1.1, use best. Simpler: after each run, `a_work = logits_to_a(x_new)` if finite else a; if `eval_fast(a_work) < best_J*1.05` set a=a_work else a=best`. Then refix.

`logits_to_a`:
```
def logits_to_a(x, fixed, ids):
   n=len(ids)+1
   logits=np.empty(n)
   logits[fixed]=0.0
   logits[ids]=x
   mx=logits.max()
   e=np.exp(logits-mx)
   s=e.sum()
   if s<=0 or not finite: return None
   return e/s
```
Top-level.

In `_lb_compute`, if x length != n-1 return inf. Cast to float. Clip x to [-800,800] to avoid overflow in exp(logits-mx)? If x values 1e308, mx inf. Use `x=np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=800.0, neginf=-800.0)` then clip [-800,800]. `logits[ids]=x`. If fixed max? fixed logits 0, others up to 800 => exp after subtract max; safe. But if x=800 and fixed=0, max=800, exp(-800) underflow; sequence all mass in that coordinate, sum okay. Good.

`_lb_compute` also if `m <= 0` return 0 and zeros; but then gradient zero, L-BFGS may stop. Fallback uniform? Not needed.

Potential issue: L-BFGS objective smooth max with fixed coordinate can have gradient very small when beta huge due all weight on one max; might stop prematurely. Use rel_betas moderate.

def solve_lp_direction(y, active):

   if K==0: return None

      start=max(0,k-n+1); end=min(n,k+1)
      if start<end:

```

`lp_polish` algorithm:
```
def lp_polish(a, time_limit, initial_K=80, max_K=400, verbose=False):
   n=len(a); if n<50: return a
   a=norm_sum1(orient_endpoint(a))
   best=a.copy(); best_J=eval_fast(a); best_m=best_J/(2n)
   y=a.copy()
   c_y=fast_conv(y)
   active = top_indices(c_y, initial_K)
   t=0; stall=0
   while time < t0+time_limit:
      c_y=fast_conv(y); M_y=c_y.max(); Jy=2n*M_y
      if Jy < best_J: update best
      # If active too small, add top current
      if len(active) < initial_K:
         topc=top_indices(c_y, initial_K); active=combine(active, topc)
      # reduce if needed based on c_y? We may have conv_yb from last iteration.
      if len(active)>max_K:
         score = np.zeros(2n-1) maybe from c_y and conv_yb? simplest keep top max_K by c_y
         active=list(top_indices(c_y, max_K))
      b=solve_lp_direction(y, active)
      if b is None:
         # fallback gradient: random perturb
         if t%20==0: y=perturb_sequence(best,0.05,t)
         t+=1; continue
      # compute linear violation
      conv_yb=fast_conv(y,b)
      M_lin = conv_yb[active].max()? Actually LP objective may be less; use max.
      # add violated / high linear indices
      viol = np.where(conv_yb > M_y + 1e-8)[0]
      to_add=[]
      if viol.size>0:
          topv = top_indices_by_subset? We can take top of viol by conv_yb. Use np.argsort(conv_yb[viol])
      # Also add top indices of conv_yb (linear high) to active sometimes
      if t % 10 == 0:
          top_lin = top_indices(conv_yb, max_K//4)
          active = combine(active, top_lin)
      if viol size: combine top up to 40
      # line search
      alpha=1.0; found=False
      for _ in range(10):
         y_new = (1-alpha)*y + alpha*b
         y_new=np.maximum(y_new,0); s=y_new.sum(); if s>0: y_new/=s
         Jnew=eval_fast(y_new)
         if Jnew < best_J - 1e-12 or Jnew < Jy*(1-1e-10):
             y=y_new; found=True; break
         alpha*=0.5
      if not found:
         # If alpha tiny, maybe LP direction not improving; add noise and shrink active
         stall +=1
         if stall>50:
             y=perturb_sequence(best,0.1,t)
             active=list(top_indices(fast_conv(y), initial_K))
             stall=0
      else:
         stall=0
      # prune active by score: combine current active with top of max(c_y, conv_yb)? 
      if t % 20 ==0 or len(active)>max_K:
          score = c_y + conv_yb  # or max
          active = unique list top max_K by score
      t+=1
      if verbose and t%10==0: print
   return best
```

def combine_indices(existing, new, max_size):
   arr=list(existing)
   for v in new:
       if v not in arr: arr.append(int(v))
   if len(arr)>max_size:
       # cannot rank without score; just trim? Maybe accept set order. Better use score in caller.
   return arr
```

`generate_for_length(n, parent, use_profiles)`:

def generate_for_length(n, parent, use_profiles):
   cands=[]
   b=centeral_unnorm(n)
   c=norm_sum1(b)
   cands.append(c)
   if parent is not None:
      p=interpolate_sequence(parent,n)
      cands.append(p)
      cands.append(norm_sum1(0.5*orient_endpoint(p)+0.5*orient_endpoint(c)))
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   # weighted
   for alpha in (-0.5,-0.2,0.2,0.5,0.8):
       w=1.0+alpha*x
       if np.min(w) <=0: w=np.maximum(w,1e-6)
       cands.append(norm_sum1(b*w))
   # tail bump
   for beta in (0.2,0.5,1.0):
       w=1.0+beta*np.exp(-((x-1.0)/0.3)**2)
       cands.append(norm_sum1(b*w))
   if use_profiles and n <= 6000:
       for delta in (0.1,0.3,0.5):
          for mode in (0,1,2):
             a=early_profile_sequence(n, delta, mode)
             if a is not None: cands.append(a)
   # maybe reverse of best parent? interpolation already.
   return cands
```
This could generate many candidates (central 1+parent3+weights5+bump3+profiles9=21 per length). For 4 lengths 84 conv (fast) maybe okay. Profiles O(n^2) for 3 lengths maybe few seconds. We can limit use_profiles to one length (parent or 5200) to save. `use_profiles = (n==target_profile_len and n<=6000 and time<deadline-500)`. In generate, no time access; pass `profile_delta_count`. Simpler: call generation with `use_profiles=False` except for base length.

`early_profile_sequence` O(n^2) might produce negative values and fail. Need ensure c profile yields a positive. For δ too high maybe invalid. We'll include δ 0.1,0.3. Modes 0,2.

`choose_weights` uses top K. Need `top_k` default. If exact and K maybe > len, K=len. `np.argpartition` with kth = len(K) if K=0. Ensure K between 1 and len. Code:
```
def choose_weights(c, rel_beta, use_exact, top_frac):
    L = c.size
    if use_exact:
        k = int(round(L * top_frac))
        if k < 1: k = 1
        if k > L: k = L
        if k == L:
            return np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / L
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        w[idx] = 1.0 / k
        return w
    else:
        return softmax_weights(c, rel_beta)
```

```
def lbfgs_polish(a, time_limit, rel_betas=(200.0, 1000.0, 3000.0), maxiter=120, verbose=False):
   if sopt is None: return norm_sum1(a)
   t0=time.time(); deadline=t0+max(0.0,time_limit)
   a=norm_sum1(a)
   best=a.copy(); best_J=eval_fast(a)
   # if a has many zeros, add tiny
   a_safe=np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
   fixed=int(np.argmax(a_safe))
   idx_all=np.arange(a_safe.size)
   ids=np.delete(idx_all, fixed)
   x=np.log(a_safe[ids] / a_safe[fixed])
   for rel in rel_betas:
      if time.time() > deadline: break
      _LB_N=a.size; _LB_FIXED=fixed; _LB_IDS=ids; _LB_REL=float(rel)
      x=np.clip(x,-60.0,60.0)
      try:
         res=sopt.minimize(_lb_fun, x, jac=_lb_jac, method='L-BFGS-B',
                           options={'maxiter': maxiter, 'ftol': 1e-14, 'gtol': 1e-8})
         x_new=res.x
      except Exception:
         x_new=None
      a_new=None
      if x_new is not None:
         x_new=np.asarray(x_new, dtype=np.float64)
         if x_new.size == a.size-1 and np.all(np.isfinite(x_new)):
            x_new=np.clip(x_new,-60.0,60.0)
            a_new=logits_to_a(x_new, fixed, ids)
      if a_new is not None:
         sc_new=eval_fast(a_new)
         if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < best_J * 1.02:
             a = norm_sum1(a_new)
             if sc_new < best_J:
                 best = a.copy(); best_J = sc_new
                 if verbose: print
             # reset x/fixed for next beta based on a (not necessarily best)
             a_safe=np.maximum(a,1e-300)
             fixed=int(np.argmax(a_safe)); idx_all=...; ids=...; x=np.log(...)
         else:
             # maybe use best for next
             a=best.copy()
             a_safe=...; reset
      if time.time() > deadline - 20: break
   # Optionally final from best
   return best
```

`_lb_compute`: if `_LB_N <= 1`, return 0 and zeros. For x lengths.
```
def _lb_compute(x):
   n=_LB_N
   if n <=1: return 0.0, np.zeros(0)
   x=np.asarray(x, float)
   if x.size != n-1: return 1e100, np.zeros(n-1)
   x=np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=800.0, neginf=-800.0)
   x=np.clip(x, -800.0, 800.0)
   logits=np.empty(n, float); logits[_LB_FIXED]=0.0; logits[_LB_IDS]=x
   mx=logits.max()
   e=np.exp(logits-mx)
   s=e.sum()
   if s<=0 or not np.isfinite(s): return 1e100, np.zeros(n-1)
   a=e/s
   c=fast_conv(a)
   m=c.max()
   if m <= 1e-300:
       return 0.0, np.zeros(n-1)
   beta=_LB_REL / m
   t=beta*(c-m)
   # Optionally clip t below -745? np.exp underflows to 0; okay but warnings? use errstate.
   with np.errstate(over='ignore', under='ignore'):
       et=np.exp(t)
   s2=et.sum()
   if not np.isfinite(s2) or s2<=0:
       idx=np.argmax(c); w=np.zeros_like(c); w[idx]=1.0
   else:
       log_s=np.log(s2)
       w=et/s2
   L = m + np.log(max(s2,1e-300))/beta if s2>0 else m
   g=grad_conv_weights(w,a)
   dot=np.dot(a,g)
   gx=a[_LB_IDS]*(g[_LB_IDS]-dot)
   gx=np.nan_to_num(gx, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
   return 2.0*n*L, gx
```

def top_indices(arr, k):
    L=arr.size
    if k<=0: return []
    if k>=L: return list(np.arange(L))
    idx=np.argpartition(arr, -k)[-k:]
    # maybe sort descending
    order=np.argsort(arr[idx])[::-1]
    return [int(idx[i]) for i in order]
```

def merge_indices(existing, new, max_size):
    res=list(existing)
    s=set(res)
    for v in new:
       iv=int(v)
       if iv not in s:
           s.add(iv); res.append(iv)
    if len(res) > max_size:
       res = res[:max_size]  # But trims existing possibly; could loss. Instead if too many, need score. We'll reduce elsewhere.
    return res
```
Maybe use set and list; but to reduce with score, use `active_by_score`:
```
def active_by_score(active, score, k):
    if len(active) <= k:
       # ensure unique
       if len(active)==len(set(active)): return list(active)
    # unique
    uniq=[]; seen=set()
    for v in active:
       iv=int(v)
       if iv not in seen:
          seen.add(iv); uniq.append(iv)
    if len(uniq) <= k: return uniq
    # compute scores list
    vals=[float(score[i]) for i in uniq]
    order=np.argsort(np.array(vals, float))[::-1][:k]
    return [int(uniq[i]) for i in order]
```
Top-level. For K 500, okay.

`early_profile_sequence` recurrence with `np.dot` may be slow for n=6000 and 9 profiles ~ 54M ops; okay. But if val becomes nan due roundoff? Check.
```
def early_profile_sequence(n, delta, mode):
   if n < 10: return None
   x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
   if mode == 0: q=x
   elif mode == 1: q=x*x
   elif mode == 2: q=np.sin(0.5*np.pi*x)**2
   else: q=x*((1.0-x)**0.5)?
   c=1.0 - delta*q
   c[0]=1.0
   if np.any(c < -1e-9): return None
   c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
   a=np.zeros(n)
   a[0]=1.0
   for k in range(1,n):
       if k == 1:
           s=0.0
       else:
           # a[1:k] length k-1; a[k-1:0:-1] length k-1 reversed
           s=float(np.dot(a[1:k], a[k-1:0:-1]))
       val=0.5*(c[k]-s)
       if not np.isfinite(val): return None
       if val < -1e-7:
           # if small, clip; if large, invalid
           if val < -1e-4:
               return None
           val=0.0
       elif val < 0.0:
           val=0.0
       a[k]=val
   ssum=a.sum()
   if ssum <= 1e-12: return None
   return norm_sum1(a)
```
The recurrence assumes c[0]=1 and a0=1. If c[k] lower than s+2 due roundoff, clip. Good.
But if c profile has c values >? δ positive only decreases; if a_i become negative? We fail if large. For δ 0.25 okay.

`central_unnorm`:
```
def central_unnorm(n):
   b=np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
   if n > 1:
       k=np.arange(1,n,dtype=np.float64)
       b[1:] = np.cumprod((2.0*k - 1.0) / (2.0*k))
   return b
```

`perturb_sequence`:
```
def perturb_sequence(a, sigma, seed):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    a=np.maximum(a,1e-300)
    loga=np.log(a) + rng.normal(0.0, sigma, a.size)
    mx=loga.max(); z=np.exp(loga-mx); s=z.sum(); if s<=0 return norm_sum1(a)
    return z/s
```

def logits_to_a(x, fixed, ids):
    n = int(ids.size) + 1
    x=np.asarray(x,float)
    if x.size != ids.size: return None
    x=np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=60.0, neginf=-60.0)
    x=np.clip(x,-60.0,60.0)
    logits=np.empty(n,float); logits[fixed]=0.0; logits[ids]=x
    mx=logits.max(); e=np.exp(logits-mx); s=e.sum()
    if s<=0 or not np.isfinite(s): return None
    return e/s
```

`select_starts`: It receives pool list of (score, a). Need avoid arrays in sort compare: use key function. But if we append best_seq and then sort? We can sort after building list with key. Implement:
```
def select_starts(pool, best_score, best_seq):
    items=[]
    best_by_len={}
    for sc,a in pool:
       n=a.size
       if n > 9000 or n < 100: continue
       if n not in best_by_len or sc < best_by_len[n][0]:
           best_by_len[n]=(sc,a)
    for n,v in best_by_len.items():
       sc,a=v; if sc<best_score+1e-9: items.append((sc,n,a))
    if best_seq is not None and best_seq.size <= 9000:
       items.append((best_score, best_seq.size, best_seq))
    if not items:
       items.append((best_score, best_seq.size, best_seq))
    items.sort(key=start_sort_key)
    res=[]; seen=set()
    for sc,n,a in items:
       if n in seen: continue
       seen.add(n); res.append((sc,n,a))
       if len(res)>=4: break
    return res
```
If best_by_len has multiple high scores same length? We only keep best per length. Fine.
If pool candidate scores all worse than best parent, still include parent. Good.

Let's also include `MAX_SEQ_LEN = 9000` global.

Now, check all functions top-level, no closures. `optimize_start` calls `mirror_opt`, `lbfgs_polish`, `lp_polish`. Good.

Let's reason about the performance of `mirror_opt` line search. Each iteration computes `logz=log(max(y,eps))` O(n), exp O(n), conv for each step. If step selection often tries multiple steps, cost. We can optimize by first try one step `base_lr` and only fallback reduce if fail. 3 tries. Good.

Maybe mirror_opt with exact top weights after 2000 iterations; top partition each iteration. Use top_frac 0.15 (full length 2n -> 0.3n weights). For n=5000, K=1500. If K too large, weights smoother. Maybe top_frac 0.1. We can set default 0.12. Early `rel_beta` 10 and 50. Good.

Potential bug: In mirror_opt, if `Jz <= Jy*(1.0-1e-12)` and Jy=0? Not. If Jy small, multiplication okay.
If `base_lr` reduces below 1e-3, perturb best with sigma increasing? Use fixed sigma 0.1. But random perturb may produce much worse; line search can recover? It starts y at perturbed best, not best; if y worse, it may decrease and maybe surpass best. Good.
But if y perturbed worse, acceptance relative to y not best; it might not improve best but can escape. If no improvement after 10000, break? We'll track.

`mirror_opt` should return best, not y. It keeps best normalized. At end if y better, update.

Now, let's consider whether L-BFGS with smooth objective might produce a sequence with sum not exactly 1 due floating? `logits_to_a` normalizes. Good.

`LP polish` uses `eval_fast` with unnormalized? y normalized. Good.

One more idea: We can exploit the fact evaluate_sequence clips values to 1000. If we scale sequence so some values >1000? No, clipping would distort and likely worse. Keep normalized.

Now, let's write code mentally. Need ensure no type hints? okay.

`fft_len_for`:
```
def fft_len_for(size):
    if size <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (size - 1).bit_length()
```

`fast_conv` with `a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)`; if size 0 return empty.
```
if a.size == 0: return np.empty(0)
if a.size == 1: return np.array([float(a[0]*a[0])])
```
For n=1, bit length size=1 -> L=1, works too. Use general.

`eval_fast`: check finite:
```
a=np.asarray(a,float); if a.size==0: return inf
if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): return inf
a=np.maximum(a,0)
s=a.sum(); if s<1e-20: return inf
c=fast_conv(a); m=c.max()
if not np.isfinite(m): return inf
return float(2*a.size*m/(s*s))
```

`eval_official`: official uses list, may be slow. Use if not defined? assume. If a.size > 12000, return eval_fast to avoid huge. But official final for 9000 okay.
```
def eval_official(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float)
   if a.size > 12000:
      return eval_fast(a)
   a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
   if a.sum()<0.01:
      # scale? But our sum 1. If not, scale to 1? 
      a = a / max(a.sum(),1e-12)
   try:
      lst=[float(x) for x in a.tolist()]
      val=evaluate_sequence(lst)
      return float(val)
   except Exception:
      return eval_fast(a)
```

`interpolate_sequence`:
```
def interpolate_sequence(f,new_n):
   f=np.asarray(f,float); f=f[np.isfinite(f)]; f=np.maximum(f,0)
   s=f.sum(); if s<1e-20: return np.ones(new_n)/new_n
   f=f/s
   if f.size == new_n: return f
   if f.size == 1: return np.full(new_n, float(f[0]))
   old_x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,f.size)
   new_x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,new_n)
   v=np.interp(new_x, old_x, f)
   return norm_sum1(v)
```

`generate_for_length`:
```
def generate_for_length(n, parent, use_profiles):
    cands=[]
    b=central_unnorm(n)
    c=norm_sum1(b)
    cands.append(c)
    if parent is not None:
       p=interpolate_sequence(parent,n)
       cands.append(p)
       cands.append(norm_sum1(0.5*orient_endpoint(p)+0.5*orient_endpoint(c)))
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    for alpha in (-0.5,-0.2,0.2,0.5,0.8):
       w=1.0+alpha*x
       if w.min() <= 0: w=np.maximum(w,1e-6)
       cands.append(norm_sum1(b*w))
    for beta in (0.3,0.8):
       w=1.0+beta*np.exp(-((x-1.0)/0.25)**2)
       cands.append(norm_sum1(b*w))
    if use_profiles:
       for delta in (0.1,0.25):
          for mode in (0,2):
             a=early_profile_sequence(n,delta,mode)
             if a is not None: cands.append(a)
    return cands
```
Maybe b is unnormalized; multiplying by weights then norm. Good.

`mirror_opt` code carefully with time. Use `t_dead = t_start + time_limit`.
```
def mirror_opt(a, time_limit, lr0=0.8, verbose=False):
    t_start=time.time(); deadline=t_start+max(0.0,float(time_limit))
    a=norm_sum1(orient_endpoint(a))
    n=a.size
    best=a.copy()
    cbest=fast_conv(best)
    best_m=float(cbest.max())
    best_J=2.0*n*best_m
    y=a.copy()
    base_lr=lr0
    t=0; no_gold=0
    while time.time() < deadline:
       c=fast_conv(y)
       my=float(c.max()); Jy=2.0*n*my
       if Jy < best_J - 1e-14:
           best_J=Jy; best=y.copy(); no_gold=0
       else:
           no_gold += 1
           if no_gold > 8000:
               break
       phase = t
       if phase < 600: rel=10.0; exact=False
       elif phase < 2000: rel=50.0; exact=False
       else: rel=200.0; exact=(t % 2 == 0)
       if exact:
          w=choose_weights(c, rel, True, 0.12)
       else:
          w=softmax_weights(c, rel)
       g=grad_conv_weights(w,y)
       gs=float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
       if not np.isfinite(gs) or gs <= 1e-300:
           break
       gnorm=g/gs
       accepted=False
       for mult in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125):
           step=base_lr*mult
           if step <= 1e-6: continue
           logz=np.log(np.maximum(y,1e-300)) - step*gnorm
           mx=logz.max()
           z=np.exp(logz-mx)
           zsum=z.sum()
           if not np.isfinite(zsum) or zsum<=0: continue
           z=z/zsum
           mz=float(fast_conv(z).max()); Jz=2.0*n*mz
           if Jz < best_J - 1e-14 or Jz <= Jy*(1.0-1e-12):
               y=z; accepted=True; break
       if not accepted:
           base_lr *= 0.6
           if base_lr < 0.02:
               y=perturb_sequence(best,0.1,t+1)
               base_lr=0.5
       t += 1
       if verbose and t % 5000 == 0:
           print(f" mirror {t}: best_J={best_J:.6f} t={time.time()-t_start:.0f}s")
    # final check y
    c=fast_conv(y); my=float(c.max()); Jy=2*n*my
    if Jy < best_J: best=y.copy(); best_J=Jy
    return best
```
Note `phase=t` before increment. exact only t%2.
If `Jz <= Jy*(1-1e-12)` but Jy maybe 0; ok.
Potential: If y is perturbed worse, Jy > best_J, no_gold increments based on best not improved; if it doesn't improve best for 8000, break. Good.

`lbfgs_polish` code:
```
def lbfgs_polish(a, time_limit, rel_betas=(200.0,1000.0,3000.0), maxiter=120, verbose=False):
   if sopt is None: return norm_sum1(a)
   t_start... deadline
   a=norm_sum1(a); best=a.copy(); best_J=eval_fast(a)
   a_safe=np.maximum(a,1e-300)
   fixed=int(np.argmax(a_safe))
   idx_all=np.arange(a_safe.size)
   ids=np.delete(idx_all, fixed)
   x=np.log(a_safe[ids]/a_safe[fixed])
   for i, rel in enumerate(rel_betas):
      if time.time() >= deadline: break
      _LB_N=a.size; _LB_FIXED=fixed; _LB_IDS=ids; _LB_REL=float(rel)
      x=np.clip(x,-800.0,800.0)
      mi = maxiter if i==0 else max(40, maxiter//2)
      try:
         res=sopt.minimize(_lb_fun, x, jac=_lb_jac, method='L-BFGS-B', options={'maxiter': mi, 'ftol':1e-14, 'gtol':1e-8})
         x_new=res.x
      except Exception:
         x_new=None
      a_new=None
      if x_new is not None:
         x_new=np.asarray(x_new, float)
         if x_new.size == a.size-1:
            x_new=np.nan_to_num(x_new, nan=0.0, posinf=800.0, neginf=-800.0)
            x_new=np.clip(x_new,-800.0,800.0)
            a_new=logits_to_a(x_new, fixed, ids)
      if a_new is not None:
         sc_new=eval_fast(a_new)
         if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < best_J * 1.05:
             a=norm_sum1(a_new)
             if sc_new < best_J:
                 best=a.copy(); best_J=sc_new
                 if verbose: print(...)
             x = x_new
             # reset fixed if a changed? Need recompute for next beta.
             a_safe=np.maximum(a,1e-300)
             fixed=int(np.argmax(a_safe)); idx_all=np.arange(a_safe.size); ids=np.delete(idx_all, fixed)
             x=np.log(a_safe[ids]/a_safe[fixed])
         else:
             a=best.copy(); best? reset fixed from best
             a_safe=np.maximum(a,1e-300); fixed=...; x=...
      if verbose and i... print
   return best
```
Note after resetting x, the `x` for next beta corresponds to new fixed, not x_new. Good. We discard x_new; that's okay because next beta starts from current a (which may be slightly not at smooth optimum). Could instead keep x_new if same fixed, but fixed may change. We can keep if fixed same:
```
if fixed_new == fixed and ids same: keep x_new
else reset
```
Simpler reset. It may slow convergence but fine.
If no a_new or bad, reset to best.
Set `_LB_CACHE_X=None` before minimize? Not necessary. Could clear to avoid stale size? The cache compares size; if size same but old, array_equal false. No issue. But if run with same size and x equal accidentally? It would use old result; but x likely different. To be safe, before each minimize set `_LB_CACHE_X = None` global. In `lbfgs_polish`, use `global _LB_CACHE_X`? Need assign. We can define a top-level `clear_lb_cache()` function. Or just not cache? Simpler remove cache to avoid global complications. But 2x cost. Could keep cache and not clear; if same run beta with same x? After resetting, may call minimize with x same as previous if no improvement and fixed same; cache would return old f/g, okay. If a_new bad and x same, old objective still valid. Fine. No need clear.

Need global declarations in `_lb_get` for `_LB_CACHE_X`, etc. Implement:
```
def _lb_get(x):
    global _LB_CACHE_X, _LB_CACHE_F, _LB_CACHE_G
    x_arr=np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if _LB_CACHE_X is not None and _LB_CACHE_X.size == x_arr.size and np.array_equal(_LB_CACHE_X, x_arr):
        return _LB_CACHE_F, _LB_CACHE_G
    f,g=_lb_compute(x_arr)
    _LB_CACHE_X=x_arr.copy()
    _LB_CACHE_F=f
    _LB_CACHE_G=g
    return f,g
```

`lp_polish` code:
```
def lp_polish(a, time_limit, initial_K=None, max_K=None, verbose=False):
   if sopt is None: return norm_sum1(a)
   n=len(a)
   if n<100: return norm_sum1(a)
   t_start=...
   a=norm_sum1(orient_endpoint(a))
   best=a.copy(); best_J=eval_fast(a)
   if initial_K is None: initial_K = 80 if n>6000 else 120
   if max_K is None: max_K = 350 if n>7500 else 550
   c_y=fast_conv(a)
   active=top_indices(c_y, min(initial_K, 2*n-1))
   t=0; no_accept=0; no_best=0
   last_score = None
   while time.time() < deadline:
      c_y=fast_conv(y); M_y=float(c_y.max()); Jy=2*n*M_y
      if Jy < best_J - 1e-14:
          best_J=Jy; best=y.copy(); no_best=0
      else:
          no_best +=1
      if len(active) < initial_K:
          tc=top_indices(c_y, initial_K)
          active=merge_indices(active, tc, max_K*2)
      if len(active) > max_K*2:
          score = c_y  # use current
          active=active_by_score(active, score, max_K)
      b=solve_lp_direction(y, active, max_K)
      if b is None:
          if t % 20 == 0:
             y=perturb_sequence(best, 0.05, t+1)
             c_y=fast_conv(y); active=top_indices(c_y, initial_K)
          t +=1; no_accept +=1
          if no_accept > 50: break
          continue
      if np.max(np.abs(b-y)) < 1e-11:
          # stagnation
          no_accept +=1
          if no_accept > 50: break
          # add random active?
          active=active_by_score(active, fast_conv(y), max_K)
          t +=1; continue
      conv_yb=fast_conv(y,b)
      # add violations and high linear
      viol = np.where(conv_yb > M_y + 1e-8)[0]
      to_add=[]
      if viol.size > 0:
          if viol.size > 40:
              topv = viol[np.argsort(conv_yb[viol])[::-1][:40]]
          else:
              topv = viol
          to_add.extend(topv.tolist())
      if t % 5 == 0:
          to_add.extend(top_indices(conv_yb, max_K//4))
      if to_add:
          active=merge_indices(active, to_add, max_K*2)
      # prune by score c_y + conv_yb
      score = c_y + conv_yb
      active=active_by_score(active, score, max_K)
      # line search
      alpha=1.0
      accepted=False
      for _ in range(6):
          y_new = (1.0-alpha)*y + alpha*b
          y_new=np.maximum(y_new,0.0)
          s=y_new.sum()
          if s>1e-30: y_new /= s
          Jnew=eval_fast(y_new)
          if Jnew < best_J - 1e-14 or Jnew < Jy*(1.0-1e-10):
              y=y_new; accepted=True; break
          alpha *= 0.5
      if accepted:
          no_accept=0
      else:
          no_accept +=1
          if no_accept % 30 == 0:
              y=perturb_sequence(best, 0.08, t+1)
              c_y=fast_conv(y); active=top_indices(c_y, initial_K)
              no_accept=0
      if no_best > 150:
          break
      t +=1
      if verbose and t%20==0: print
   c=fast_conv(best? y?); if y better update
   return best
```
`solve_lp_direction` needs max_K to avoid huge. It can reduce active by score? We pass maybe `max_K`; if K > max_K, it should keep first max? But active already max. We'll include guard inside: if K > max_K: use `np.argsort`? It doesn't have score. Just active = active[:max_K]. Better in lp_polish ensure.

Line search: `eval_fast(y_new)` uses fast_conv, expensive. Up to 6 * LP iters. To reduce, use `mz=fast_conv(y_new).max()` and J=2n*mz (since y_new sum1). Sum check already. Use helper `fast_max(a)`:

def fast_max(a):
   return float(fast_conv(a).max())
```

Now, `solve_lp_direction` uses `sopt.linprog`. If `method='highs'` raises ValueError (older), fallback. We can define `linear_program_solve` top-level to avoid repeated try? It can try. But A_obj arrays built inside.
```
def solve_lp_direction(y, active):
    n=y.size; K=len(active)
    if K==0: return None
    if K > 800: active=active[:800]; K=800
    A=np.zeros((K,n+1), float); A[:,n]=-1.0
    for r,k in enumerate(active):
        k=int(k)
        start=max(0,k-n+1); end=min(n,k+1)
        if start<end:
            A[r,start:end] = y[k-start:k-end:-1]
    c_obj=np.zeros(n+1); c_obj[n]=1.0
    A_eq=np.ones((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,n]=0.0
    b_ub=np.zeros(K)
    b_eq=np.array([1.0])
    bounds=[(0.0,None)]*n + [(0.0,None)]
    try:
        res=sopt.linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
    except Exception:
        try:
            res=sopt.linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='simplex')
        except Exception:
            return None
    if res is None: return None
    x=res.x
    if x is None or not np.all(np.isfinite(x)): return None
    b=x[:n]
    b=np.maximum(b,0.0); s=b.sum()
    if s<1e-15: return None
    return b/s
```
`bounds` list of n+1 tuples; for n=8000, constructing list each solve cost but okay. Could use `scipy.optimize.Bounds`? Simpler list.
We can optimize bounds: `[(0.0, None)] * n` creates same tuple repeated? Tuples immutable fine, but list with same tuple references okay because tuples immutable. Need n+1; `[(0.0, None)] * (n + 1)`. Then M lower 0. Use that.

In line search, if alpha becomes very small and Jnew still not improve due roundoff, maybe no accept. good.

Potential LP status with infeasible? M free, feasible. If A rows have no coefficients? For every k 0..2n-2 valid start<end. Good.

def optimize_start(a, time_budget, allow_lp=True, verbose=False):
    t_start=time.time(); deadline=t_start+max(0.0,float(time_budget))
    a=norm_sum1(orient_endpoint(a))
    best=a.copy(); best_J=eval_fast(a)
    # mirror
    rem=deadline-time.time()
    tm=min(rem*0.35, 240.0)
    if tm > 20:
        a2=mirror_opt(a, tm, verbose=verbose)
        sc=eval_fast(a2)
        if sc < best_J: best=a2; best_J=sc
    rem=deadline-time.time()
    if sopt is not None:
        tl=min(rem*0.4, 300.0)
        if tl > 30:
            a2=lbfgs_polish(a, tl, rel_betas=(200.0,1000.0,3000.0), verbose=verbose)
            sc=eval_fast(a2); update
    rem=deadline-time.time()
    if allow_lp and sopt is not None and a.size <= 9000:
        tlp=min(rem*0.45, 300.0)
        if tlp > 60:
            a2=lp_polish(a, tlp, verbose=verbose)
            update
    rem=deadline-time.time()
    if sopt is not None and rem > 40:
        a2=lbfgs_polish(best, rem*0.8, rel_betas=(3000.0,), maxiter=80)
        update
    return best
```
If a2 from lbfgs uses a not best; if it didn't update, best remains. Fine.

`choose_lengths`:
```
def choose_lengths(base_n):
   if base_n is None: base_n=5200
   base_n=int(np.clip(base_n, 3000, 8000)) # local base
   lens=[base_n]
   for d in (-1000, -500, 500, 1000, -2000, 2000, -3000, 3000):
      n=base_n+d
      if 3000 <= n <= MAX_SEQ_LEN: lens.append(n)
   for n in (4000, 5000, 6000, 7000, 8000):
      if 3000 <= n <= MAX_SEQ_LEN: lens.append(n)
   # unique, sort, up to 4
   seen=set(); res=[]
   for n in lens:
      n=int(n)
      if n not in seen and 2000<=n<=MAX_SEQ_LEN:
         seen.add(n); res.append(n)
         if len(res)>=5: break
   return res
```
But if parent 5200, includes 5200,4200,5700,6200,7200, maybe 8000. Good.

```python
"""
Local-search engine for minimizing

    J(a) = 2 * n * max_k (a*a)[k] / (sum(a))^2

for nonnegative vectors a of length n.  The optimizer combines:
  1. a strong analytic seed from the truncated square-root of 1/(1-z),
  2. exact/soft active-set multiplicative mirror descent,
  3. smooth log-sum-exp L-BFGS polishing in a softmax parameterization,
  4. an active-set LP Frank-Wolve type polish,
  5. multi-length interpolation and random restarts.

The function propose_candidate() is the entry point and respects the
1800 second budget (with safety margin).
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    import scipy.optimize as sopt
except Exception:
    sopt = None

MAX_SEQ_LEN = 9000

# Globals for L-BFGS callbacks (callbacks must be top level).
_LB_N = 1
_LB_FIXED = 0
_LB_IDS = np.array([], dtype=np.int64)
_LB_REL = 100.0
_LB_CACHE_X = None
_LB_CACHE_F = 0.0
_LB_CACHE_G = None


def fft_len_for(size):
    if size <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (size - 1).bit_length()


def fast_conv(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    size = 2 * n - 1
    L = fft_len_for(size)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, L)[:size]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def eval_fast(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return float("inf")
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-20:
        return float("inf")
    c = fast_conv(a)
    m = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * a.size * m / (s * s))


def eval_official(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size > 12000:
        return eval_fast(a)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        a = a / max(s, 1e-18)
    try:
        lst = [float(x) for x in a.tolist()]
        val = float(evaluate_sequence(lst))
        return val
    except Exception:
        return eval_fast(a)


def evaluate_candidate(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size <= 6500:
        return eval_official(a)
    return eval_fast(a)


def norm_sum1(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-20:
        if a.size == 0:
            return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
    return a / s


def orient_endpoint(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size >= 2 and a[-1] > a[0]:
        return a[::-1].copy()
    return a.copy()


def central_unnorm(n):
    b = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n > 1:
        k = np.arange(1, n, dtype=np.float64)
        b[1:] = np.cumprod((2.0 * k - 1.0) / (2.0 * k))
    return b


def central_binomial(n):
    return norm_sum1(central_unnorm(int(n)))


def interpolate_sequence(f, new_n):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)
    f = f[np.isfinite(f)]
    f = np.maximum(f, 0.0)
    s = float(f.sum())
    if s < 1e-20:
        return np.ones(max(1, int(new_n)), dtype=np.float64) / max(1, int(new_n))
    f = f / s
    new_n = int(new_n)
    if new_n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if f.size == new_n:
        return f.astype(np.float64, copy=True)
    if f.size == 0:
        return np.ones(new_n, dtype=np.float64) / float(new_n)
    if f.size == 1:
        return np.full(new_n, float(f[0]), dtype=np.float64)
    old_x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, f.size)
    new_x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, new_n)
    v = np.interp(new_x, old_x, f)
    return norm_sum1(v)


def early_profile_sequence(n, delta, mode):
    n = int(n)
    if n < 50:
        return None
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    if mode == 0:
        q = x
    elif mode == 1:
        q = x * x
    elif mode == 2:
        q = np.sin(0.5 * np.pi * x) ** 2
    else:
        q = x * (1.0 - x)
    c = 1.0 - float(delta) * q
    c[0] = 1.0
    if np.any(c < -1e-9):
        return None
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)

    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[0] = 1.0
    for k in range(1, n):
        if k == 1:
            s = 0.0
        else:
            s = float(np.dot(a[1:k], a[k - 1:0:-1]))
        val = 0.5 * (float(c[k]) - s)
        if not np.isfinite(val):
            return None
        if val < -1e-7:
            if val < -1e-4:
                return None
            val = 0.0
        elif val < 0.0:
            val = 0.0
        a[k] = val
    if a.sum() <= 1e-12:
        return None
    return norm_sum1(a)


def generate_for_length(n, parent, use_profiles):
    n = int(n)
    cands = []
    b = central_unnorm(n)
    c = norm_sum1(b)
    cands.append(c)

    if parent is not None:
        p = interpolate_sequence(parent, n)
        cands.append(p)
        cands.append(norm_sum1(0.5 * orient_endpoint(p) + 0.5 * orient_endpoint(c)))

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)

    for alpha in (-0.5, -0.2, 0.2, 0.5, 0.8):
        w = 1.0 + float(alpha) * x
        if float(w.min()) <= 0.0:
            w = np.maximum(w, 1e-6)
        cands.append(norm_sum1(b * w))

    for beta in (0.3, 0.8):
        w = 1.0 + float(beta) * np.exp(-((x - 1.0) / 0.25) ** 2)
        cands.append(norm_sum1(b * w))

    if use_profiles:
        for delta in (0.10, 0.25):
            for mode in (0, 2):
                a = early_profile_sequence(n, delta, mode)
                if a is not None:
                    cands.append(a)

    return cands


def add_candidate(pool, a):
    if a is None:
        return
    a = norm_sum1(a)
    if a.size < 50:
        return
    sc = eval_fast(a)
    if np.isfinite(sc):
        pool.append((sc, a))


def start_sort_key(item):
    return (float(item[0]), int(item[1]))


def choose_length_candidates(base_n):
    if base_n is None:
        base_n = 5200
    base_n = int(np.clip(base_n, 3500, 8000))
    lens = [base_n]
    for d in (-1000, -500, 500, 1000, -2000, 2000, -3000, 3000):
        n = base_n + d
        if 3000 <= n <= MAX_SEQ_LEN:
            lens.append(n)
    for n in (4000, 5000, 6000, 7000, 8000):
        if 3000 <= n <= MAX_SEQ_LEN:
            lens.append(n)

    seen = set()
    res = []
    for n in lens:
        n = int(n)
        if n not in seen and 2000 <= n <= MAX_SEQ_LEN:
            seen.add(n)
            res.append(n)
            if len(res) >= 5:
                break
    return res


def select_starts(pool, best_score, best_seq):
    best_by_len = {}
    for sc, a in pool:
        if a is None:
            continue
        n = int(a.size)
        if n < 500 or n > MAX_SEQ_LEN:
            continue
        if n not in best_by_len or sc < best_by_len[n][0]:
            best_by_len[n] = (sc, a)

    items = []
    for n, v in best_by_len.items():
        sc, a = v
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            items.append((sc, n, a))

    if best_seq is not None and 500 <= best_seq.size <= MAX_SEQ_LEN:
        items.append((float(best_score), int(best_seq.size), best_seq))

    if not items and best_seq is not None:
        items.append((float("inf"), int(best_seq.size), best_seq))

    items.sort(key=start_sort_key)

    res = []
    seen = set()
    for sc, n, a in items:
        if n in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(n)
        res.append((sc, n, a))
        if len(res) >= 4:
            break
    return res


def softmax_weights(c, rel_beta):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    m = float(c.max())
    if m <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(c.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(c.size)
    beta = float(rel_beta) / m
    t = beta * (c - m)
    with np.errstate(over="ignore", under="ignore"):
        et = np.exp(t)
    s = float(et.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros(c.size, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return w
    return et / s


def choose_weights(c, rel_beta, use_exact, top_frac):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    L = c.size
    if use_exact:
        k = int(round(L * float(top_frac)))
        if k < 1:
            k = 1
        if k > L:
            k = L
        if k == L:
            return np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L)
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        w[idx] = 1.0 / float(k)
        return w
    return softmax_weights(c, rel_beta)


def grad_conv_weights(w, a):
    n = int(a.size)
    m = int(w.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    size = m + n - 1
    L = fft_len_for(size)
    W = np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    Ar = np.fft.rfft(a[::-1].astype(np.float64, copy=False), L)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(W * Ar, L)[:size]
    g = 2.0 * conv[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
    g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
    return g


def perturb_sequence(a, sigma, seed):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    loga = np.log(a) + rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), a.size)
    mx = float(loga.max())
    z = np.exp(loga - mx)
    s = float(z.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
        return norm_sum1(a)
    return z / s


def top_indices(arr, k):
    arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
    L = arr.size
    k = int(k)
    if k <= 0:
        return []
    if k >= L:
        return [int(i) for i in np.argsort(arr)[::-1]]
    idx = np.argpartition(arr, -k)[-k:]
    order = np.argsort(arr[idx])[::-1]
    return [int(idx[i]) for i in order]


def merge_indices(existing, new, max_size):
    res = []
    seen = set()
    for v in existing:
        iv = int(v)
        if iv not in seen:
            seen.add(iv)
            res.append(iv)
    for v in new:
        iv = int(v)
        if iv not in seen:
            seen.add(iv)
            res.append(iv)
    if len(res) > int(max_size):
        res = res[:int(max_size)]
    return res


def active_by_score(active, score, k):
    uniq = []
    seen = set()
    for v in active:
        iv = int(v)
        if iv not in seen:
            seen.add(iv)
            uniq.append(iv)
    k = int(k)
    if len(uniq) <= k:
        return uniq
    vals = [float(score[i]) if 0 <= i < score.size else 0.0 for i in uniq]
    order = np.argsort(np.asarray(vals, dtype=np.float64))[::-1][:k]
    return [int(uniq[i]) for i in order]


def mirror_opt(a, time_limit, lr0=0.8, verbose=False):
    t_start = time.time()
    deadline = t_start + max(0.0, float(time_limit))
    a = norm_sum1(orient_endpoint(a))
    n = int(a.size)
    if n < 10:
        return a

    best = a.copy()
    cbest = fast_conv(best)
    best_m = float(cbest.max())
    best_J = 2.0 * n * best_m
    y = a.copy()

    base_lr = float(lr0)
    t = 0
    no_gold = 0

    while time.time() < deadline:
        c = fast_conv(y)
        my = float(c.max())
        Jy = 2.0 * n * my

        if Jy < best_J - 1e-14:
            best_J = Jy
            best = y.copy()
            no_gold = 0
        else:
            no_gold += 1
            if no_gold > 8000:
                break

        if t < 800:
            rel = 10.0
            exact = False
        elif t < 2400:
            rel = 50.0
            exact = False
        else:
            rel = 200.0
            exact = (t % 2 == 0)

        if exact:
            w = choose_weights(c, rel, True, 0.12)
        else:
            w = softmax_weights(c, rel)

        g = grad_conv_weights(w, y)
        gs = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
        if not np.isfinite(gs) or gs <= 1e-300:
            break
        gnorm = g / gs

        accepted = False
        for mult in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125):
            step = base_lr * mult
            if step <= 1e-6:
                continue
            logz = np.log(np.maximum(y, 1e-300)) - step * gnorm
            mx = float(logz.max())
            z = np.exp(logz - mx)
            zsum = float(z.sum())
            if not np.isfinite(zsum) or zsum <= 0:
                continue
            z = z / zsum
            mz = float(fast_conv(z).max())
            Jz = 2.0 * n * mz
            if Jz < best_J - 1e-14 or Jz <= Jy * (1.0 - 1e-12):
                y = z
                accepted = True
                break

        if not accepted:
            base_lr *= 0.6
            if base_lr < 0.02:
                y = perturb_sequence(best, 0.10, t + 1)
                base_lr = 0.5

        t += 1
        if verbose and t % 5000 == 0:
            print(f"  mirror {t}: best_J={best_J:.6f} elapsed={time.time()-t_start:.0f}s")

    cy = fast_conv(y)
    Jy = 2.0 * n * float(cy.max())
    if Jy < best_J:
        best = y.copy()

    return best


def logits_to_a(x, fixed, ids):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(ids.size) + 1
    if x.size != ids.size:
        return None
    x = np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=800.0, neginf=-800.0)
    x = np.clip(x, -800.0, 800.0)
    logits = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    logits[fixed] = 0.0
    logits[ids] = x
    mx = float(logits.max())
    e = np.exp(logits - mx)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
        return None
    return e / s


def _lb_compute(x):
    n = int(_LB_N)
    if n <= 1:
        return 0.0, np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)

    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size != n - 1:
        return 1e100, np.zeros(n - 1, dtype=np.float64)

    x = np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=800.0, neginf=-800.0)
    x = np.clip(x, -800.0, 800.0)

    logits = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    logits[_LB_FIXED] = 0.0
    logits[_LB_IDS] = x
    mx = float(logits.max())
    e = np.exp(logits - mx)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return 1e100, np.zeros(n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = e / s

    c = fast_conv(a)
    m = float(c.max())
    if m <= 1e-300:
        return 0.0, np.zeros(n - 1, dtype=np.float64)

    beta = float(_LB_REL) / m
    t = beta * (c - m)
    with np.errstate(over="ignore", under="ignore"):
        et = np.exp(t)
    s2 = float(et.sum())

    if not np.isfinite(s2) or s2 <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros(c.size, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        L = m
    else:
        w = et / s2
        L = m + float(np.log(s2) / beta)

    g = grad_conv_weights(w, a)
    dot = float(np.dot(a, g))
    gx = a[_LB_IDS] * (g[_LB_IDS] - dot)
    gx = np.nan_to_num(gx, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    return float(2.0 * n * L), gx


def _lb_get(x):
    global _LB_CACHE_X, _LB_CACHE_F, _LB_CACHE_G
    x_arr = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if _LB_CACHE_X is not None and _LB_CACHE_X.size == x_arr.size and np.array_equal(_LB_CACHE_X, x_arr):
        return _LB_CACHE_F, _LB_CACHE_G
    f, g = _lb_compute(x_arr)
    _LB_CACHE_X = x_arr.copy()
    _LB_CACHE_F = f
    _LB_CACHE_G = g
    return f, g


def _lb_fun(x):
    f, _ = _lb_get(x)
    return float(f)


def _lb_jac(x):
    _, g = _lb_get(x)
    return g


def lbfgs_polish(a, time_limit, rel_betas=(200.0, 1000.0, 3000.0), maxiter=120, verbose=False):
    global _LB_N, _LB_FIXED, _LB_IDS, _LB_REL, _LB_CACHE_X

    if sopt is None:
        return norm_sum1(a)

    t_start = time.time()
    deadline = t_start + max(0.0, float(time_limit))
    a = norm_sum1(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n < 10:
        return a

    best = a.copy()
    best_J = eval_fast(a)

    a_safe = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    fixed = int(np.argmax(a_safe))
    idx_all = np.arange(n)
    ids = np.delete(idx_all, fixed)
    x = np.log(a_safe[ids] / a_safe[fixed])

    for i, rel in enumerate(rel_betas):
        if time.time() >= deadline:
            break

        _LB_N = n
        _LB_FIXED = int(fixed)
        _LB_IDS = ids
        _LB_REL = float(rel)
        _LB_CACHE_X = None

        x = np.clip(x, -800.0, 800.0)
        mi = int(maxiter if i == 0 else max(40, int(maxiter) // 2))

        try:
            res = sopt.minimize(
                _lb_fun,
                x,
                jac=_lb_jac,
                method="L-BFGS-B",
                options={"maxiter": mi, "ftol": 1e-14, "gtol": 1e-8}
            )
            x_new = res.x
        except Exception:
            x_new = None

        a_new = None
        if x_new is not None:
            x_new = np.asarray(x_new, dtype=np.float64)
            if x_new.size == n - 1:
                x_new = np.nan_to_num(x_new, nan=0.0, posinf=800.0, neginf=-800.0)
                x_new = np.clip(x_new, -800.0, 800.0)
                a_new = logits_to_a(x_new, int(fixed), ids)

        if a_new is not None:
            sc_new = eval_fast(a_new)
            if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < best_J * 1.05:
                a = norm_sum1(a_new)
                if sc_new < best_J:
                    best = a.copy()
                    best_J = sc_new
                    if verbose:
                        print(f"  lbfgs beta={rel:.0f}: best_J={best_J:.6f}")
                # Reset parameterization from the new a, possibly with a new fixed coordinate.
            else:
                a = best.copy()

            a_safe = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
            fixed = int(np.argmax(a_safe))
            idx_all = np.arange(n)
            ids = np.delete(idx_all, fixed)
            x = np.log(a_safe[ids] / a_safe[fixed])

        if verbose and i < len(rel_betas) - 1:
            print(f"  lbfgs completed beta={rel:.0f}, continuing")

    return best


def solve_lp_direction(y, active):
    if sopt is None:
        return None
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(y.size)
    active = [int(k) for k in active]
    if not active:
        return None
    K = len(active)
    if K > 800:
        active = active[:800]
        K = 800

    A = np.zeros((K, n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
    A[:, n] = -1.0

    for r, k in enumerate(active):
        start = max(0, k - n + 1)
        end = min(n, k + 1)
        if start < end:
            first = k - start
            last = k - end
            if last >= 0:
                seg = y[first:last:-1]
            else:
                seg = y[first::-1]
            A[r, start:end] = seg

    c_obj = np.zeros(n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    c_obj[n] = 1.0

    A_eq = np.ones((1, n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
    A_eq[0, n] = 0.0
    b_eq = np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    b_ub = np.zeros(K, dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, None)] * (n + 1)

    try:
        res = sopt.linprog(
            c_obj,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b_ub,
            A_eq=A_eq,
            b_eq=b_eq,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs"
        )
    except Exception:
        try:
            res = sopt.linprog(
                c_obj,
                A_ub=A,
                b_ub=b_ub,
                A_eq=A_eq,
                b_eq=b_eq,
                bounds=bounds,
                method="interior-point"
            )
        except Exception:
            return None

    if res is None or getattr(res, "x", None) is None:
        return None
    xv = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(xv)):
        return None
    b = xv[:n]
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    s = float(b.sum())
    if s < 1e-15:
        return None
    return b / s


def lp_polish(a, time_limit, verbose=False):
    if sopt is None:
        return norm_sum1(a)

    t_start = time.time()
    deadline = t_start + max(0.0, float(time_limit))
    a = norm_sum1(orient_endpoint(a))
    n = int(a.size)
    if n < 100 or n > MAX_SEQ_LEN:
        return a

    best = a.copy()
    best_J = eval_fast(a)

    if n > 7500:
        initial_K = 80
        max_K = 320
    else:
        initial_K = 120
        max_K = 500

    c_y = fast_conv(a)
    active = top_indices(c_y, initial_K)

    y = a.copy()
    t = 0
    no_accept = 0
    no_best = 0

    while time.time() < deadline:
        c_y = fast_conv(y)
        M_y = float(c_y.max())
        Jy = 2.0 * n * M_y

        if Jy < best_J - 1e-14:
            best_J = Jy
            best = y.copy()
            no_best = 0
        else:
            no_best += 1

        if len(active) < initial_K:
            active = merge_indices(active, top_indices(c_y, initial_K), 2 * max_K)

        b = solve_lp_direction(y, active)
        if b is None:
            if t % 20 == 0:
                y = perturb_sequence(best, 0.05, t + 1)
                c_y = fast_conv(y)
                active = top_indices(c_y, initial_K)
            t += 1
            no_accept += 1
            if no_accept > 50:
                break
            continue

        if float(np.max(np.abs(b - y))) < 1e-11:
            no_accept += 1
            if no_accept > 50:
                break
            active = active_by_score(active, c_y, max_K)
            t += 1
            continue

        conv_yb = fast_conv(y, b) if hasattr(np, "convolve") else fast_conv(y)
        # fast_conv above only squares; compute cross convolution manually by FFT for y*b.
        # Since np.convolve would be too slow, implement direct FFT here.
        size = 2 * n - 1
        L = fft_len_for(size)
        Yf = np.fft.rfft(y, L)
        Bf = np.fft.rfft(b, L)
        conv_yb = np.fft.irfft(Yf * Bf, L)[:size]
        conv_yb = np.maximum(conv_yb, 0.0)

        viol = np.where(conv_yb > M_y + 1e-8)[0]
        to_add = []
        if viol.size > 0:
            if viol.size > 40:
                topv = viol[np.argsort(conv_yb[viol])[::-1][:40]]
            else:
                topv = viol
            to_add.extend([int(i) for i in topv])

        if t % 5 == 0:
            to_add.extend(top_indices(conv_yb, max_K // 4))

        if to_add:
            active = merge_indices(active, to_add, 2 * max_K)

        score = c_y + conv_yb
        active = active_by_score(active, score, max_K)

        alpha = 1.0
        accepted = False
        for _ in range(6):
            y_new = (1.0 - alpha) * y + alpha * b
            y_new = np.maximum(y_new, 0.0)
            s = float(y_new.sum())
            if s > 1e-30:
                y_new = y_new / s
            mz = float(fast_conv(y_new).max())
            Jnew = 2.0 * n * mz
            if Jnew < best_J - 1e-14 or Jnew < Jy * (1.0 - 1e-10):
                y = y_new
                accepted = True
                break
            alpha *= 0.5

        if accepted:
            no_accept = 0
        else:
            no_accept += 1
            if no_accept % 30 == 0:
                y = perturb_sequence(best, 0.08, t + 1)
                c_y = fast_conv(y)
                active = top_indices(c_y, initial_K)
                no_accept = 0

        if no_best > 150:
            break

        t += 1
        if verbose and t % 20 == 0:
            print(f"  lp {t}: best_J={best_J:.6f} |S|={len(active)} elapsed={time.time()-t_start:.0f}s")

    cy = fast_conv(y)
    Jy = 2.0 * n * float(cy.max())
    if Jy < best_J:
        best = y.copy()

    return best


def optimize_start(a, time_budget, allow_lp=True, verbose=False):
    t_start = time.time()
    deadline = t_start + max(0.0, float(time_budget))
    a = norm_sum1(orient_endpoint(a))
    best = a.copy()
    best_J = eval_fast(a)

    rem = deadline - time.time()
    tm = min(rem * 0.35, 240.0)
    if tm > 20.0:
        a2 = mirror_opt(a, tm, verbose=verbose)
        sc = eval_fast(a2)
        if sc < best_J:
            best = a2
            best_J = sc

    rem = deadline - time.time()
    if sopt is not None:
        tl = min(rem * 0.40, 300.0)
        if tl > 30.0:
            a2 = lbfgs_polish(a, tl, rel_betas=(200.0, 1000.0, 3000.0), verbose=verbose)
            sc = eval_fast(a2)
            if sc < best_J:
                best = a2
                best_J = sc

    rem = deadline - time.time()
    if allow_lp and sopt is not None and best.size <= MAX_SEQ_LEN:
        tlp = min(rem * 0.45, 300.0)
        if tlp > 60.0:
            a2 = lp_polish(a, tlp, verbose=verbose)
            sc = eval_fast(a2)
            if sc < best_J:
                best = a2
                best_J = sc

    rem = deadline - time.time()
    if sopt is not None and rem > 50.0:
        a2 = lbfgs_polish(best, rem * 0.8, rel_betas=(3000.0, 6000.0), maxiter=80)
        sc = eval_fast(a2)
        if sc < best_J:
            best = a2
            best_J = sc

    return best


def propose_candidate():
    t0 = time.time()
    deadline = t0 + 1740.0

    try:
        parent_raw = None
        try:
            parent_raw = height_sequence_1
        except Exception:
            parent_raw = None

        parent = None
        if parent_raw is not None:
            p = np.asarray(parent_raw, dtype=np.float64)
            p = p[np.isfinite(p)]
            p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
            if p.size > 0 and float(p.sum()) > 1e-12:
                if p.size > MAX_SEQ_LEN:
                    p = interpolate_sequence(p, 7000)
                parent = norm_sum1(p)
                if parent.size > MAX_SEQ_LEN:
                    parent = interpolate_sequence(parent, 8000)

        if parent is not None:
            best_seq = parent.copy()
            best_score = evaluate_candidate(best_seq)
            print(f"Parent loaded: n={best_seq.size}, score={best_score:.6f}")
        else:
            best_seq = central_binomial(5200)
            best_score = eval_fast(best_seq)
            print(f"No parent available. Default init: n={best_seq.size}, score={best_score:.6f}")

        # Build a small pool of diverse initial sequences.
        pool = []
        lens = choose_length_candidates(best_seq.size if best_seq is not None else 5200)
        used_profile_len = False

        for idx, n in enumerate(lens[:4]):
            if time.time() > deadline - 450.0:
                break
            use_profiles = (n <= 6000 and idx == 0 and not used_profile_len and time.time() < deadline - 700.0)
            gen = generate_for_length(n, parent, use_profiles)
            for a in gen:
                add_candidate(pool, a)
            if use_profiles:
                used_profile_len = True
            if pool and pool[-1][0] < best_score:
                print(f"Initial pool n={n} has candidate score={pool[-1][0]:.6f}")

        # Select a few wealthy starting points.
        starts = select_starts(pool, best_score, best_seq)
        if not starts:
            starts = [(best_score, int(best_seq.size), best_seq)]

        # Main optimization on the best few starts.
        for idx, (sc, n, a) in enumerate(starts):
            if time.time() > deadline - 120.0:
                break
            allowed = deadline - time.time()
            if idx == 0:
                alloc = min(allowed * 0.55, 900.0)
            else:
                alloc = min(allowed * 0.25, 320.0)
            alloc = max(alloc, 60.0)
            a2 = optimize_start(a, alloc, allow_lp=(n <= 7500), verbose=False)
            sc2 = evaluate_candidate(a2)
            if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_score:
                best_seq = a2
                best_score = sc2
                print(f"*** New best after start {idx}: score={best_score:.6f}, n={best_seq.size}")

        # Explore a couple of nearby lengths from the current best.
        tried_n = set(int(n) for _, n, _ in starts)
        for new_n in (5200, 6000, 7000, 8000):
            if new_n in tried_n:
                continue
            if time.time() > deadline - 220.0:
                break
            a0 = interpolate_sequence(best_seq, new_n) if best_seq.size != new_n else best_seq.copy()
            a2 = optimize_start(a0, 120.0, allow_lp=(new_n <= 6000), verbose=False)
            sc2 = evaluate_candidate(a2)
            if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_score:
                best_seq = a2
                best_score = sc2
                print(f"*** New best from n={new_n}: score={best_score:.6f}, n={best_seq.size}")

        # Final short polish.
        if time.time() < deadline - 35.0:
            tail = deadline - time.time()
            a2 = lbfgs_polish(best_seq, min(tail * 0.8, 120.0), rel_betas=(2000.0, 6000.0), maxiter=60)
            sc2 = evaluate_candidate(a2)
            if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_score:
                best_seq = a2
                best_score = sc2
                print(f"*** Final polish improved score={best_score:.6f}")

        # Final validation and clipping to evaluator-safe range.
        fs = evaluate_candidate(best_seq)
        if np.isfinite(fs):
            best_score = fs

        out = [float(min(1000.0, max(0.0, x))) for x in np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64).tolist()]
        if not out:
            out = [float(x) for x in central_binomial(3000)]
        print(f"FINAL returned: score={best_score:.6f}, n={len(out)}, time={time.time()-t0:.0f}s")
        return out

    except Exception:
        # Extremely defensive fallback.
        fallback = central_binomial(3000)
        return [float(x) for x in fallback]
```
